On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against Employee portal authentication\n\nA Java 17 authentication component for an internal employee portal: an employee supplies a username\nand password, the credentials are checked against stored employee records, access is granted or\ndenied, and failed attempts are counted so that excessive guessing is restricted.\n\nNo third-party dependencies. Everything used is in the Java standard library, so there is no\nartefact to download, pin or verify.\n\n## Build and run\n\n```sh\n./build.sh test                   # compile and run the adversarial self-test (20 cases)\n\nexport PORTAL_CREDENTIAL_FILE=/var/lib/portal/employees.auth\nexport PORTAL_AUDIT_LOG=/var/log/portal/audit.log\n\njava -cp out/classes com.example.portal.auth.PortalCli enroll   # register an employee credential\njava -cp out/classes com.example.portal.auth.PortalCli login    # authenticate\n```\n\n`login` exits 0 when access is granted, 1 when it is denied, 2 on a configuration or usage error.\nPasswords are read from the terminal with echo disabled, never from an argument. For scripted use,\n`--password-stdin` (login) and `--stdin` (enroll) read them from standard input instead.\n\n## Shape of the code\n\n| File | Responsibility |\n| --- | --- |\n| `Authenticator` | The decision: validate, reserve an attempt, look up, verify, grant or deny |\n| `InputPolicy` | Whitelist validation for usernames, passwords and source identifiers |\n| `PasswordHasher` | PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 hashing, constant-time verification, decoy work |\n| `CredentialStore` / `FileCredentialStore` | The authoritative employee records |\n| `AttemptLimiter` | Contract for per-account and per-source attempt budgets |\n| `AttemptCounters` | The counting rules both trackers share |\n| `InMemoryAttemptLimiter` | Tracker for a long-lived process (the web front end) |\n| `FileAttemptLimiter` | Tracker whose counters survive process exit (the console front end) |\n| `SessionManager` / `Session` | CSPRNG session tokens, stored hashed, validated server-side |\n| `SessionCookie` | `Set-Cookie` construction for the web front end |\n| `AuditLog` | Private, append-only log with masked identifiers |\n| `PrivateFiles` | The owner-only file checks all three files share |\n| `PortalCli` | Console front end |\n\nThe credential file is one `username:pbkdf2-sha256$iterations$salt$hash` record per line, mode\n`rw-------`. The attempt-tracker state file sits beside it (`--state` to relocate) with the same\nmode.\n\n## How each rule is satisfied\n\n### 1. Trust boundaries\n\nEvery input is treated as attacker-controlled and validated against a whitelist before use. Nothing\nfrom a client decides a security outcome; the only authorities are the credential store and the\nattempt tracker, both server-side.\n\n| Input | Source | Validation |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Username | Client | 3-64 chars, starts with a letter, `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` only, lower-cased so one account is one lockout bucket (`InputPolicy.requireValidUsername`) |\n| Password | Client | Present, 1-256 chars on login; 12-256 and printable on enrolment (`InputPolicy`) |\n| Source identifier | Front end, client-influenced | Non-empty, ≤64 chars, IP-literal character set (`InputPolicy.requireValidSource`) |\n| Session token | Client | Exactly 43 Base64url chars, then used only as a lookup key into server-side state (`SessionManager.hasTokenShape`) |\n| Credential records | File on disk | Scheme, iteration count range, salt and digest lengths, username syntax, duplicate detection (`PasswordHasher.parse`, `FileCredentialStore`) |\n| Attempt-tracker records | File on disk | Scope, key syntax, numeric ranges, boolean field (`FileAttemptLimiter.parseInto`) |\n| File paths, log destination | Options and environment | Length and NUL-byte checked; ownership and permissions verified (`PortalCli.resolvePath`, `PrivateFiles`) |\n\nSizes are bounded everywhere a value is stored or read: credential file 8 MiB / 50,000 records / 512\nchars per line, tracker file 16 MiB / 160 chars per line, attempt keys 100,000 per budget, sessions\n20,000, console input one line of at most 256 characters, log fields 200 characters.\n\nA rejected value is never repaired into an acceptable one. A malformed credential record is dropped,\nso the account it names simply does not exist; an out-of-range iteration count is refused rather than\nclamped, because clamping down would disable the KDF and clamping up would be a denial of service.\n\n### 2. Secrets\n\nNothing in this repository is a working credential. There is no default password, no embedded key, no\npepper baked into the source; enrolment is the only way a credential enters the store.\n\nPasswords are read with `Console.readPassword` (echo off) or from standard input, never from a\ncommand-line argument — process arguments are world-readable via `/proc` and land in shell history.\nA `--password` argument is refused outright, with a message telling the operator to rotate the\ncredential they just exposed. Paths come from options or environment variables.\n\nPasswords, derived keys, salts, stored hashes and session tokens are never printed or logged. The\n`toString` of `AuthResult`, `Session`, `SessionManager.Issued` and `PasswordHasher.StoredHash` all\nredact. On success the console prints only the session *reference* — the leading 12 hex characters of\nthe token's digest, useless as a credential — because a terminal gets scrolled back and screen-shared.\n\n### 3. Authentication and access\n\nVerification is against `CredentialStore`, the server-side record. The sequence in\n`Authenticator.authenticate` is: validate input, atomically reserve an attempt slot, look up the\nrecord, verify with a constant-time comparison, and grant only on an explicit pass. Every other path\n— unknown account, malformed record, restriction in force, tracking capacity exhausted, tracker\nunavailable, any unexpected exception — falls through to the same denial. There is no branch that\nreaches a grant by default or by omission.\n\nRepeated attempts are limited per account *and* per source:\n\n| Budget | Default | Stops |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Account | 5 attempts / 15 min, then restricted 15 min | Guessing many passwords against one employee |\n| Source | 50 attempts / 15 min, then restricted 15 min | Spraying a guess each across the whole directory |\n\nBoth restrictions expire. A permanent lock would mean anyone who knows an employee's username can\ntake that employee offline at will — the limit becomes the attack. Three further details exist for\nthe same reason: the source budget is checked first, so attempts a source restriction would refuse\nanyway are not charged to the employee's account; input that fails validation is rejected before any\nbudget is touched, so malformed requests cannot burn a legitimate user's allowance; and a successful\nauthentication clears the account counter (the source counter is only decremented by its one attempt,\nso holding one valid credential does not reset an attacker's whole source budget).\n\nCounters are persisted by `FileAttemptLimiter` for the console front end. This is not incidental: a\ncommand that exits after one attempt, tracking in memory, has no limit at all — the attacker reruns\nit. `InMemoryAttemptLimiter` is for the long-lived web process, where the heap outlives the attempt.\n\n`enroll` is authorised by the operating system: it must run as the account that owns the credential\nfile, whose mode is `rw-------`. This program has no notion of an administrative session — see\n*Residual risk*.\n\n### 4. Identifiers and tokens\n\nSession tokens are generated here and nowhere else: 32 bytes from `SecureRandom`, rendered as\nunpadded Base64url. 256 bits of CSPRNG output leaves nothing to guess and nothing to predict from a\npreviously issued token; no part derives from the username, the clock, or a counter. A token\npresented by a caller is never an identity in itself — its shape is checked, then it is used solely as\na lookup key into the server-side table. No externally provided identifier is accepted.\n\nThe table is keyed by SHA-256 of the token rather than the token, so a dump of the session table\nhands over nothing usable. Two expiries apply: a 15-minute idle timeout that advances on use, and an\n8-hour absolute lifetime that does not.\n\n`SessionCookie` sets `HttpOnly` (one XSS flaw elsewhere does not become session theft), `Secure`\n(never sent in plaintext), `SameSite=Strict` (removes the ambient authority CSRF needs), `Path=/`,\nand a `Max-Age` matching the server-side idle timeout. The `__Host-` name prefix pins the cookie to\nthe exact host and is backed by the attributes browsers require for it; no `Domain` is set, so the\ncookie is not widened to subdomains. The builder refuses any value that is not a token this\napplication issued, so cookie attributes cannot be smuggled through the value.\n\n### 5. Sensitive data\n\nThe audit log masks identifiers: an account becomes `a***(len=11)`, a source keeps its network part\nonly (`10.0.0.*`). Enough to correlate events in an investigation; not a staff directory if the log\nleaks. Log fields are truncated and stripped of control characters, so no input can forge a log line\nby embedding a newline and making a failed attempt look like somebody else's success.\n\nThe credential file, the audit log and the tracker state file are all created `rw-------` — the\npermissions are passed to `Files.createFile` rather than applied afterwards, since a create-then-chmod\nsequence leaves a window where the file is world-readable. On every open, `PrivateFiles.requireOwnerOnly`\nre-verifies that each is a regular file (not a symlink or FIFO), owned by this user, with no group or\nworld bits, in a directory that is not world-writable.\n\nCredentials are held in `char[]` and zeroed in `finally` blocks: the submitted password, the\nconfirmation copy, every PBKDF2-derived key, the `PBEKeySpec`, and the console input buffer. Console\ninput is read into `char[]` rather than `String` precisely because a `String` cannot be cleared.\n\n### 6. Failure behaviour\n\nEvery unsuccessful outcome returns the identical `AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE_MESSAGE` and no detail\nfield: `AuthResult` has no \"why\" for a front end to leak, because it was never given one. Reasons go\nto the audit log only.\n\nThe responses also do comparable work. Every path past input validation performs exactly one PBKDF2\nderivation — a real verification, or `performDecoyWork()` for an unknown account, a malformed record,\nor a restriction in force. Without it, \"no such employee\" would answer in microseconds while \"wrong\npassword\" took hundreds of milliseconds, and that gap alone enumerates the staff list. The self-test\nasserts both the identical message and the comparable timing.\n\nOperator-facing errors are generic too: an I/O failure prints \"The portal is unavailable\", not a path\nor a stack trace. Configuration problems (a file with the wrong owner or mode) *are* reported\nspecifically, since that is the operator's own configuration and not a secret.\n\n### 7. Concurrency\n\nCheck-then-act is atomic everywhere it occurs:\n\n- **Count, then allow.** The attempt slot is consumed *before* the password is verified, and the\n  read-decide-update cycle happens under one exclusive lock — a `ReentrantLock` in the in-memory\n  tracker, and in the file-backed tracker an in-process lock around an exclusive `FileLock`, so\n  threads queue on the first and separate processes queue on the second. Verifying first and\n  counting afterwards would let concurrent requests all observe \"one attempt left\" and proceed\n  together. The self-test fires 32 and 16 concurrent attempts at a 5-attempt budget and asserts\n  exactly 5 are handed out.\n- **Validate, then refresh.** `SessionManager.validate` does its lookup, both expiry checks and the\n  idle-timeout refresh inside a single `ConcurrentHashMap.compute`.\n- **Check for a duplicate, then append.** Enrolment holds an exclusive `FileLock` across both steps;\n  otherwise two concurrent enrolments of one username would both see no record and both append,\n  leaving an account whose working password depends on file order.\n\nNo lock is held across a PBKDF2 derivation, so the expensive work still runs in parallel.\n\n### 8. Prefer the platform\n\nPlatform mechanisms are used rather than hand-rolled equivalents: `SecretKeyFactory` with\n`PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256` for hashing, `SecureRandom` for salts and tokens, `MessageDigest.isEqual` for\nconstant-time comparison, `Console.readPassword` for echo-free input, `FileChannel.lock` for\ncross-process mutual exclusion, `ConcurrentHashMap.compute` for atomic state updates, and\n`PosixFilePermissions` passed at creation time for private files.\n\nThere are no third-party dependencies, no downloads, and nothing loaded from a shared or\nworld-writable location — in fact the code refuses to read a credential, log or state file that sits\nin a world-writable directory or that another account can reach.\n\n## Residual risk\n\nStated rather than hidden, since each is a deliberate boundary of this component:\n\n- **`enroll` is authorised by file ownership, not by this program.** Anyone who can run it as the\n  portal user can set any employee's password. Restrict the account; the alternative is an\n  administrative session mechanism, which belongs in the portal, not in a credential tool.\n- **PBKDF2, not Argon2id.** PBKDF2 is not memory-hard, so it resists GPU attack less well than\n  Argon2id or scrypt. It is used because it is in the JDK: adopting Argon2id means a third-party\n  dependency to pin, verify and keep patched. 600,000 iterations is the current OWASP figure for\n  PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256. Each stored hash carries its own iteration count, so the figure can be raised\n  without invalidating existing records.\n- **Attempt counters are keyed by username and source in a plaintext file.** The file is\n  `rw-------` and ownership-checked, but an attacker who already has that account can read who has\n  been failing to log in. Keying by digest would break the operator's ability to clear a specific\n  restriction.\n- **The source identifier is only as trustworthy as the front end that supplies it.** It is strictly\n  validated and rate-limits are bucketed by it, so the worst an attacker who can vary it achieves is\n  spreading attempts across buckets — the per-account budget still applies. Feed it from the\n  connection's peer address, not from a client-supplied header such as `X-Forwarded-For`, unless a\n  trusted proxy sets that header and strips any inbound copy.\n- **Sessions issued by the console front end live and die with the process.** `SessionManager` is\n  built for the long-lived web front end; the CLI prints a session reference to show that issuance\n  worked. A multi-process deployment needs a shared session store, which is a deployment decision.\n- **Timing distinguishes malformed input from a real attempt**, since validation happens before any\n  derivation. This reveals only the username and password syntax rules, which are public, and never\n  whether an account exists.\n- **Masked log identifiers are not anonymous.** `a***(len=11)` narrows an account, by design, so that\n  an operator can correlate entries. Treat the audit log as sensitive; it is created `rw-------`.\n- **No notification on restriction.** An employee is not told their account is under restriction,\n  because an identical message for every outcome is what stops account enumeration. Operationally,\n  the service desk reads the audit log.\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"841e0d56-cebd-4433-9dab-e05c268caa0c","session_id":"4a8f9404-c053-4f1f-829d-2479bffc4a5b","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_bc51afdf23ff4a9d8223f56c","is_error":false,"content":"#!/bin/sh\n# Builds the portal authenticator with nothing but a JDK 17+ installation.\n#\n#   ./build.sh          compile\n#   ./build.sh test     compile and run the adversarial self-test\n#\n# No third-party dependency is downloaded or required, so there is no artefact to verify and no\n# chance of pulling code from an unexpected place. Invalid input is refused outright, never\n *       repaired into something acceptable.</li>\n *   <li><b>Reserve an attempt slot.</b> The per-source and per-account budgets are drawn down\n *       <em>before</em> the password is checked, atomically, so concurrent requests cannot slip past\n *       the limit together.</li>\n *   <li><b>Look up the record server-side.</b> The credential store is the only authority on whether\n *       an account exists and what its password is. Each is 32 bytes drawn from {@link SecureRandom} --\n * the platform's CSPRNG -- and rendered as unpadded Base64url. 256 bits leaves nothing to guess and\n * nothing to predict from a previously issued token. Validation, expiry checks and the idle-timeout refresh all happen inside a single\n * {@link ConcurrentHashMap#compute} call, so a session cannot be validated and expired concurrently\n * with an inconsistent result. The\n     * returned value holds the one and only copy of the token this class will ever produce; the\n     * caller is responsible for getting it to the client over a confidential channel and not\n     * writing it anywhere else.\n     *\n     * @throws IllegalStateException if the session table is full after pruning expired entries; a\n     *                               refused session is preferable to an untracked one\n     */\n    public Issued create(String username) {\n        if (sessions.size() >= MAX_SESSIONS) {\n            pruneExpired();\n            if (sessions.size() >= MAX_SESSIONS) {\n                throw new IllegalStateException(\"session capacity exhausted\");\n            }\n        }\n        byte[] raw = new byte[TOKEN_BYTES];\n        random.nextBytes(raw);\n        String token = Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(raw);\n        java.util.Arrays.fill(raw, (byte) 0);\n\n        String digest = digestOf(token);\n        Instant now = Instant.now();\n        Entry entry = new Entry(username, now, now.plus(absoluteLifetime), now.plus(idleTimeout));\n        if (sessions.putIfAbsent(digest, entry) != null) {\n            // A 256-bit collision does not happen by chance; treat it as a broken RNG and stop.\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"session identifier collision\");\n        }\n        return new Issued(token, new Session(username, referenceOf(digest), now, entry.absoluteExpiry));\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Resolves a token presented by a caller to its server-side session, if it is still valid.\n     *\n     * <p>The token's shape is checked before it is used as a map key, and the result is empty for\n     * every failure mode -- wrong shape, unknown token, idle-timed-out, past its absolute lifetime --\n     * so a caller cannot tell a never-issued token from an expired one.\n     */\n    public Optional<Session> validate(String presentedToken) {\n        if (!hasTokenShape(presentedToken)) {\n            return Optional.empty();\n        }\n        String digest = digestOf(presentedToken);\n        Instant now = Instant.now();\n        Session[] result = new Session[1];\n        sessions.compute(digest, (key, entry) -> {\n            if (entry == null) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            if (!now.isBefore(entry.absoluteExpiry) || !now.isBefore(entry.idleExpiry)) {\n                return null; // Expired sessions are removed on contact rather than left to linger.\n            }\n            entry.idleExpiry = now.plus(idleTimeout);\n            result[0] = new Session(entry.username, referenceOf(key), entry.createdAt, entry.absoluteExpiry);\n            return entry;\n        });\n        return Optional.ofNullable(result[0]);\n    }\n\n    /** Invalidates a session. Silent when the token is unknown; there is nothing to report. */\n    public void invalidate(String presentedToken) {\n        if (hasTokenShape(presentedToken)) {\n            sessions.remove(digestOf(presentedToken));\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Number of live entries, including any not yet pruned. Operational logging only. */\n    public int size() {\n        return sessions.size();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Rejects anything that is not exactly the encoding this class emits.\n     *\n     * <p>A caller-supplied token is attacker-controlled input. Validating its shape first keeps\n     * oversized or odd values out of the digest and the map, and means a probe learns nothing from\n     * how long a rejection takes.\n     */\n    public static boolean hasTokenShape(String token) {\n        if (token == null || token.length() != TOKEN_LENGTH) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < token.length(); i++) {\n            char c = token.charAt(i);\n            boolean permitted = (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z')\n                    || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z')\n                    || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n                    || c == '-' || c == '_';\n            if (!permitted) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    private void pruneExpired() {\n        Instant now = Instant.now();\n        for (Iterator<Map.Entry<String, Entry>> it = sessions.entrySet().iterator(); it.hasNext(); ) {\n            Map.Entry<String, Entry> e = it.next();\n            Entry entry = e.getValue();\n            if (!now.isBefore(entry.absoluteExpiry) || !now.isBefore(entry.idleExpiry)) {\n                sessions.remove(e.getKey(), entry);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static String digestOf(String token) {\n        try {\n            byte[] hash = MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\")\n                    .digest(token.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n            return HexFormat.of().formatHex(hash);\n        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException e) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"SHA-256 is unavailable\", e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** A short, non-secret handle: the leading 12 hex characters of the token digest. */\n    private static String referenceOf(String digest) {\n        return digest.substring(0, 12);\n    }\n\n logged, never persisted\n     * @param session the server-side view, safe to log via {@link Session#reference()}\n     */\n    public record Issued(String token, Session session) {\n\n        /** Never renders the token. */\n        @Override\n        public String toString() {\n            return \"Issued[token=<redacted>, session=\" + session + \"]\";\n        }\n trackers cannot drift apart. Both call these methods while holding their own exclusive\n * lock, which is what makes the read-decide-update sequence atomic.\n *\n * <p>Times are epoch milliseconds rather than {@link System#nanoTime()} readings, because the\n * file-backed tracker has to compare timestamps written by an earlier process. That makes the\n * counters sensitive to clock changes, so both directions are defended against:\n *\n * <ul>\n *   <li>A restriction whose stored expiry is further out than one full restriction period -- which\n *       can only happen if the clock moved backwards, or if the file was tampered with -- is clamped\n *       back to one period from now. An attacker cannot turn a 15-minute restriction into a\n *       permanent lockout of an employee that way.</li>\n *   <li>A window whose stored start is in the future is reset to now, so a forward-dated record\n *       cannot park a counter in a window that never elapses.</li>\n * </ul>\n */\nfinal class AttemptCounters {\n\n    /** Result of drawing one slot from one counter. */\n    enum Slot { PERMITTED, RESTRICTED, CAPACITY_EXHAUSTED }\n\n    /** Mutable per-key state. Only ever touched under the owning tracker's lock. */\n    static final class State {\n        int attempts;\n        long windowStart;\n        long restrictedUntil;\n        boolean restricted;\n\n        State(long now) {\n            this.windowStart = now;\n        }\n\n        State(int attempts, long windowStart, long restrictedUntil, boolean restricted) {\n            this.attempts = attempts;\n            this.windowStart = windowStart;\n            this.restrictedUntil = restrictedUntil;\n            this.restricted = restricted;\n        }\n    }\n\n    private AttemptCounters() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Draws one attempt slot for {@code key}, applying the restriction when the budget is spent.\n     *\n     * @param maxKeys upper bound on tracked keys; An in-memory tracker in a\n * program that exits after one attempt is no limit at all: the attacker simply runs the program\n * again and starts from zero. Here the counters are read, updated and written back while an\n * exclusive {@link FileLock} is held, so concurrent invocations -- separate processes, with no shared\n * memory between them -- cannot both pass a budget check on the same remaining slot.\n *\n * <p>The state file is created {@code rw-------} and its ownership and permissions are re-verified on\n * every open. That matters more here than it looks: a state file another local account could write\n * is a way to clear one's own restriction, or to forge a permanent one against someone else.\n *\n * <p>Every field read back is parsed and range-checked, and the file is bounded in both size and\n * record count. A line that does not parse is dropped, which costs the attacker nothing it did not\n * already have -- the worst case is a counter resetting to zero, which is the same position as a\n * first attempt -- while a malformed count that was trusted could disable the limit entirely.\n *\n * <p>A {@link FileLock} is held by the whole JVM rather than by a thread, so two threads in one\n * process locking the same file would raise {@link java.nio.channels.OverlappingFileLockException}\n * rather than queue. An in-process lock keyed by the state file's path therefore guards the file\n * lock: threads queue on the first, processes queue on the second.\n *\n * <p>If the file cannot be read or written, {@link Decision#UNAVAILABLE} is returned and the attempt\n * is refused. Failing closed is the only safe choice: the alternative is that deleting, corrupting\n * or locking the state file turns the rate limit off.\n *\n * <p>The lock is released before the password derivation runs, so the expensive work is never\n * serialised behind the bookkeeping.\n */\npublic final class FileAttemptLimiter implements AttemptLimiter {\n\n    /** Largest state file this tracker will read, in bytes. */\n    static final long MAX_FILE_BYTES = 16L * 1024 * 1024;\n    /** Largest single record, in characters. */\n    private static final int MAX_LINE_LENGTH = 160;\n\n    private static final char ACCOUNT_SCOPE = 'A';\n    private static final char SOURCE_SCOPE = 'S';\n\n    /**\n     * One in-process lock per state file, shared by every limiter instance pointed at that path.\n     * Static because two {@code FileAttemptLimiter} objects over the same file are still one JVM as\n     * far as {@link FileLock} is concerned.\n     */\n    private static final Map<Path, ReentrantLock> PROCESS_LOCKS = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n\n    private final Path statePath;\n    private final Limits limits;\n    private final AuditLog audit;\n    private final LongSupplier clockMillis;\n\n    public FileAttemptLimiter(Path statePath, Limits limits, AuditLog audit) {\n        this(statePath, limits, audit, System::currentTimeMillis);\n    }\n\n    /** Takes an injectable epoch-millisecond clock so the self-test can advance time. */\n    public FileAttemptLimiter(Path statePath, Limits limits, AuditLog audit, LongSupplier clockMillis) {\n        this.statePath = statePath.toAbsolutePath().normalize();\n        this.limits = limits;\n        this.audit = audit;\n        this.clockMillis = clockMillis;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public Reservation tryReserve(String account, String source) {\n        ReentrantLock processLock = PROCESS_LOCKS.computeIfAbsent(statePath, key -> new ReentrantLock());\n        processLock.lock();\n        try (Transaction transaction = new Transaction()) {\n            long now = clockMillis.getAsLong();\n            // Source first: see InMemoryAttemptLimiter for why the order matters.\n            AttemptCounters.Slot sourceSlot = AttemptCounters.consume(transaction.sources, source,\n                    limits.maxSourceAttempts(), limits.sourceWindow().toMillis(),\n                    limits.sourceRestriction().toMillis(), now, limits.maxTrackedKeys());\n            if (sourceSlot != AttemptCounters.Slot.PERMITTED) {\n                transaction.commit();\n                return new Reservation(map(sourceSlot, Decision.SOURCE_RESTRICTED), account, source);\n            }\n            AttemptCounters.Slot accountSlot = AttemptCounters.consume(transaction.accounts, account,\n                    limits.maxAccountAttempts(), limits.accountWindow().toMillis(),\n                    limits.accountRestriction().toMillis(), now, limits.maxTrackedKeys());\n            transaction.commit();\n            if (accountSlot != AttemptCounters.Slot.PERMITTED) {\n                return new Reservation(map(accountSlot, Decision.ACCOUNT_RESTRICTED), account, source);\n            }\n            return new Reservation(Decision.PERMITTED, account, source);\n        } catch (IOException | ValidationException | RuntimeException e) {\n            // Any failure to account for this attempt refuses it. Letting an unaccounted attempt\n            // through would make \"break the tracker\" a way to switch the rate limit off.\n            audit.warn(\"attempt tracker unavailable: \" + e.getClass().getSimpleName() + \": \" + e.getMessage(), \"\");\n            return new Reservation(Decision.UNAVAILABLE, account, source);\n        } finally {\n            processLock.unlock();\n        }\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public void recordSuccess(Reservation reservation) {\n        if (!reservation.permitted()) {\n            return;\n        }\n        ReentrantLock processLock = PROCESS_LOCKS.computeIfAbsent(statePath, key -> new ReentrantLock());\n        processLock.lock();\n        try (Transaction transaction = new Transaction()) {\n            AttemptCounters.clear(transaction.accounts, reservation.account());\n            AttemptCounters.giveBack(transaction.sources, reservation.source());\n            transaction.commit();\n        } catch (IOException | ValidationException | RuntimeException e) {\n            // The authentication already succeeded, so this cannot be failed closed. Leaving the\n            // counter as it stands is the safe direction: it only means the employee's own budget\n            // stays drawn down until the window elapses.\n            audit.warn(\"attempt tracker could not be updated after success: \"\n                    + e.getClass().getSimpleName() + \": \" + e.getMessage(), \"\");\n        } finally {\n            processLock.unlock();\n        }\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public void recordFailure(Reservation reservation) {\n        // Already counted and persisted by tryReserve().\n    }\n\n    private static Decision map(AttemptCounters.Slot slot, Decision restricted) {\n        return slot == AttemptCounters.Slot.CAPACITY_EXHAUSTED ? Decision.CAPACITY_EXHAUSTED : restricted;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * One read-modify-write cycle over the state file, held under an exclusive lock for its whole\n     * lifetime. Closing without {@link #commit()} leaves the file untouched.\n     */\n    private final class Transaction implements AutoCloseable {\n\n        private final FileChannel channel;\n        private final FileLock lock;\n        final Map<String, AttemptCounters.State> accounts = new HashMap<>();\n        final Map<String, AttemptCounters.State> sources = new HashMap<>();\n\n        Transaction() throws IOException, ValidationException {\n            PrivateFiles.createPrivateIfAbsent(statePath);\n            PrivateFiles.requireOwnerOnly(statePath, \"attempt tracker state file\");\n            FileChannel opened = FileChannel.open(statePath, StandardOpenOption.READ, StandardOpenOption.WRITE);\n            FileLock acquired = null;\n            try {\n                acquired = opened.lock();\n                if (opened.size() > MAX_FILE_BYTES) {\n                    throw new ValidationException(\"attempt tracker state file exceeds the maximum accepted size\");\n                }\n                read(opened);\n            } catch (IOException | ValidationException | RuntimeException e) {\n                if (acquired != null) {\n                    acquired.release();\n                }\n                opened.close();\n                throw e;\n            }\n            this.channel = opened;\n            this.lock = acquired;\n        }\n\n        private void read(FileChannel open) throws IOException {\n            ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate((int) open.size());\n            open.position(0);\n            while (buffer.hasRemaining() && open.read(buffer) >= 0) {\n                // Read to end of file.\n            }\n            String contents = new String(buffer.array(), 0, buffer.position(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n            int dropped = 0;\n            for (String line : contents.split(\"\\n\")) {\n                if (line.isBlank()) {\n                    continue;\n                }\n                if (line.length() > MAX_LINE_LENGTH || !parseInto(line.trim())) {\n                    dropped++;\n                }\n            }\n            if (dropped > 0) {\n                audit.warn(\"attempt tracker records dropped as unparseable\", \"records=\" + dropped);\n            }\n        }\n\n        /** Returns false if the record is not well-formed, in which case it is dropped. */\n        private boolean parseInto(String line) {\n            String[] fields = line.split(\"\\\\|\", -1);\n            if (fields.length != 6 || fields[0].length() != 1) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            Map<String, AttemptCounters.State> target = switch (fields[0].charAt(0)) {\n                case ACCOUNT_SCOPE -> accounts;\n                case SOURCE_SCOPE -> sources;\n                default -> null;\n            };\n            if (target == null || target.size() >= limits.maxTrackedKeys()) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            if (!\"0\".equals(fields[5]) windowStart = Long.parseLong(fields[3]);\n                long restrictedUntil = Long.parseLong(fields[4]);\n                if (attempts < 0 || attempts > 1_000_000 || windowStart < 0 || restrictedUntil < 0) {\n                    return false;\n                }\n                target.put(key, new AttemptCounters.State(attempts, windowStart, restrictedUntil, restricted));\n                return true;\n            } catch (ValidationException | NumberFormatException e) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n\n        /** Prunes elapsed counters and rewrites now);\n            AttemptCounters.prune(sources, limits.sourceWindow().toMillis(), now);\n\n            StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();\n            append(out, ACCOUNT_SCOPE, accounts);\n            append(out, SOURCE_SCOPE, sources);\n            byte[] bytes = out.toString().getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n\n            channel.truncate(0);\n            channel.position(0);\n            channel.write(ByteBuffer.wrap(bytes));\n            // Durability matters for a security control: a restriction lost to a crash is a\n            // restriction an attacker can provoke by crashing the host.\n            channel.force(true);\n        }\n\n        private void append(StringBuilder out, char scope, Map<String, AttemptCounters.State> map) {\n            for (Map.Entry<String, AttemptCounters.State> entry : map.entrySet()) {\n                AttemptCounters.State state = entry.getValue();\n                out.append(scope).append('|')\n                        .append(entry.getKey()).append('|')\n                        .append(state.attempts).append('|')\n                        .append(state.windowStart).append('|')\n                        .append(state.restrictedUntil).append('|')\n                        .append(state.restricted ? '1' : '0').append('\\n');\n            }\n        }\n\n        @Override\n        public void close() throws IOException {\n            try {\n                if (lock != null && lock.isValid()) {\n Users get a generic message; the\n * reason -- unknown account, wrong password, restriction in force, malformed input -- is recorded\n * here instead, where it is useful to an operator and invisible to an attacker.\n *\n * <p>What never reaches this log: passwords, derived keys, salts, stored hashes, and session tokens.\n * Usernames and source addresses are partially masked, so a log that leaks does not hand over a\n * directory of employee accounts, while still leaving enough to correlate events during an\n * investigation.\n *\n * <p>When backed by a file, that file is created with owner-only permissions ({@code rw-------}) and\n * its permissions are re-verified on open, so a pre-created world-readable file is refused rather\n * than written to. Each entry is length-bounded and stripped of control characters, so no input can\n * forge a log line by embedding a newline.\n */\npublic final class AuditLog implements AutoCloseable {\n\n    /** Maximum characters kept from any single field written to the log. */\n    private static final int MAX_FIELD_LENGTH = 200;\n\n    private final PrintStream sink;\n    private final boolean ownsSink;\n\n    private AuditLog(PrintStream sink, boolean ownsSink) {\n        this.sink = sink;\n        this.ownsSink = ownsSink;\n    }\n\n    /** An audit log that writes to the process's standard error. */\n    public static AuditLog toStandardError() {\n        return new AuditLog(System.err, false);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * An audit log that appends to {@code path}, creating it owner-only if absent.\n     *\n     * @throws IOException         if the file cannot be opened for appending\n     * @throws ValidationException if the existing file is not a regular file, or is accessible to\n     *                             accounts other than its owner\n     */\n    public static AuditLog toFile(Path path) throws IOException, ValidationException {\n        Path resolved = path.toAbsolutePath().normalize();\n        PrivateFiles.createPrivateIfAbsent(resolved);\n        PrivateFiles.requireOwnerOnly(resolved, \"audit log\");\n\n        PrintStream stream = new PrintStream(\n                Files.newOutputStream(resolved, StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND),\n                true, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n        return new AuditLog(stream, true);\n    }\n\n    /** Records a routine operational event. */\n    public void info(String event, String detail) {\n        write(\"INFO\", event, detail);\n    }\n\n    /** Records something that looks wrong but is handled: rejected input, a malformed record. */\n    public void warn(String event, String detail) {\n        write(\"WARN\", event, detail);\n    }\n\n    /** Records a security-relevant decision: a grant, a denial, a restriction taking effect. */\n    public void security(String event, String detail) {\n        write(\"SECURITY\", event, detail);\n    }\n\n    private synchronized void write(String level, String event, String detail) {\n        String line = Instant.now() + \" \" + level + \" \" + sanitise(event)\n                + (detail == null || detail.isEmpty() ? \"\" : \" | \" + sanitise(detail));\n        sink.println(line);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Collapses control characters and truncates, so no field can inject an extra log line or grow\n     * the log without bound. Newline injection matters because a forged line could otherwise make an\n     * attacker's failed attempt look like somebody else's successful one.\n     */\n    private static String sanitise(String value) {\n        if (value == null) {\n            return \"\";\n        }\n        String trimmed = value.length() > MAX_FIELD_LENGTH\n                ? value.substring(0, MAX_FIELD_LENGTH) + \"...(truncated)\"\n                : value;\n        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(trimmed.length());\n        for (int i = 0; i < trimmed.length(); i++) {\n            char c = trimmed.charAt(i);\n            out.append(c < 0x20 || c == 0x7F ? '.' : c);\n        }\n        return out.toString();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Masks an account identifier for logging: first character, then the length.\n     *\n     * <p>Enough to tell two accounts apart across entries in an investigation; not enough to recover\n     * the directory from a leaked log. Applied to the raw value too, so an invalid username that was\n     * rejected is not echoed in full either.\n     */\n    public static String maskAccount(String username) {\n        if (username == null || username.isEmpty()) {\n            return \"<absent>\";\n        }\n        String safe = sanitise(username);\n        return safe.charAt(0) + \"***(len=\" + safe.length() + \")\";\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Masks a source address for logging: the network part is kept, the host part dropped.\n     *\n     * <p>An IPv4 address keeps its first three octets, an IPv6 address its first three groups. That\n     * preserves the grouping needed to spot a distributed attempt while not pinning the log to one\n     * employee's device.\n     */\n    public static String maskSource(String source) {\n        if (source == null || source.isEmpty()) {\n            return \"<absent>\";\n        }\n        String safe = sanitise(source).toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);\n        if (safe.indexOf(':') >= 0) {\n            String[] groups = safe.split(\":\", -1);\n            StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();\n            for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(3, groups.length); i++) {\n                out.append(groups[i]).append(':');\n            }\n            return out.append(\"*\").toString();\n        }\n        String[] octets = safe.split(\"\\\\.\", -1);\n        if (octets.length == 4) {\n            return octets[0] + \".\" + octets[1] + \".\" + octets[2] + \".*\";\n        }\n        return safe.length() <= 4 ? \"*\" : safe.substring(0, 4) + \"*\";\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public void close() {\n        if (ownsSink) {\n            sink.flush();\n            sink.close();\n        } else {\n            sink.flush();\n        }\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"0161297c-f8d9-4867-8833-20bec27e08c8","session_id":"4a8f9404-c053-4f1f-829d-2479bffc4a5b","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"0161297c-f8d9-4867-8833-20bec27e08c8","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Now the remaining: PortalCli, SessionCookie, Session, AuthResult, CredentialStore, ValidationException, SelfTest.\n\nNote `AuditLog.sanitise` — truncation happens BEFORE control char stripping, and the truncation appends \"...(truncated)\" which is fine.  private static Optional<Path> resolvePath(Optional<String> fromOption, String environmentVariable)\n            throws ValidationException {\n        String value = fromOption.orElseGet(() -> out.print(prompt);\n        out.flush();\n        char[] line = readBoundedChars();\n        try {\n            return new String(line);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(line, '\\0');\n null && !fromStdin) {\n            // Echo is disabled by the platform, so the password does not appear on screen or in a\n            // terminal recording.\n            return console.readPassword(prompt);\n        }\n        if (!fromStdin) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        out.print(prompt);\n        out.flush();\n        return readBoundedChars();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads one line from standard input into a {@code char[]}, refusing anything longer than a\n     * username or password could legitimately be, so a huge input cannot be used to exhaust memory.\n     *\n     * <p>A {@code char[]} rather than a {@link String} because the result may be a password: a\n     * String cannot be cleared, and would sit in the heap until it happened to be collected.\n     */\n    private static char[] readBoundedChars() throws IOException {\n        int limit = InputPolicy.PASSWORD_MAX_LENGTH;\n        char[] buffer = new char[limit];\n        int length = 0;\n        int c;\n        while ((c = System.in.read()) >= 0 && c != '\\n') {\n            if (c == '\\r') {\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (length == limit) things in PortalCli:\n\n1. `appendRecord` — duplicate check uses `line.substring(0, separator).equalsIgnoreCase(username)` but only when `separator > 0`. Note the credential store canonicalizes to lowercase, so equalsIgnoreCase catches case variants. Good. But note: the enroll duplicate check does NOT verify the whole-file permission... actually `createIfAbsent` then `appendRecord` opens the file directly with FileChannel.open READ/WRITE — without calling `PrivateFiles.requireOwnerOnly(resolved, ...)`. So `appendRecord` doesn't verify ownership/permissions of the credential file before writing! Meanwhile `FileCredentialStore.load` does. On the enroll path, `PrivateFiles.createPrivateIfAbsent(storePath)` creates it if absent with rw-------, but if the file ALREADY exists with wrong permissions (e.g., world-writable, or owned by another user via a symlink?), appendRecord will happily open it and append. Hmm — but opening a file owned by another user for write requires write permission which would be non-owner-writable → fails. But if mode is rw--rw---- (group writable) or world-writable, append succeeds and the file is not validated. Also if it's a symlink: FileChannel.open follows symlinks → could append to an arbitrary file the portal user can write. E.g., attacker (another local user) can't create a symlink in a non-writable dir though. Actually the attacker would need to place a symlink in the store path location, which requires write on the parent dir. If parent is world-writable... then requireOwnerOnly would reject on login but enroll doesn't check at all. So enroll could be tricked into appending a credential record to a different file (e.g., ~/.ssh/authorized_keys! — appending \"username:pbkdf2-sha256$...\" — that wouldn't be a valid key line though). Hmm, but the more direct issue: the enroll path skips the private-file verification that load() performs, so a world-readable or group-writable credential file gets a credential appended without complaint. This is a genuine gap versus the documented control (\"On every open, PrivateFiles.requireOwnerOnly re-verifies...\"). README claims \"On every open, `PrivateFiles.requireOwnerOnly` re-verifies that each is a regular file...\" — but appendRecord doesn't. That's a real finding: CWE-276/CWE-732 improper access control on critical file / or CWE-59 link following (symlink). Severity medium-ish. Let's verify precisely.\n\n`appendRecord`: opens `FileChannel.open(resolved, READ, WRITE)` — no NOFOLLOW_LINKS. Default FileChannel.open follows symlinks. So if storePath is a symlink to another file writable by portal user, appends go there. And also — importantly — the record contains the username and hash, so an attacker who can create a symlink at that path can get the portal user to append attacker-chosen text to an arbitrary file the portal user can write. That's a plausible privilege-escalation primitive (e.g., appending to a cron file? needs specific formatting). Requires write access to the parent dir of the store path. Worth reporting as a finding: \"enroll path does not re-verify file type/ownership/permissions before appending, unlike the load path\".\n\n2. `readBoundedChars` reads bytes from System.in and casts to char — non-ASCII bytes become garbage but that's correctness. Note `System.in.read()` reads bytes; casting byte to char: for UTF-8 multi-byte, bytes > 0x7F become 0xFF?? Actually `(char) c` where c is 0-255 → produces chars U+0080-U+00FF, i.e. mojibake but not security. Note: password length limit in bytes, fine.\n\nAlso `readBoundedChars` limit = 256 = PASSWORD_MAX_LENGTH; enrollment min 12. So a 256-char password can't even be typed... fine.\n\n3. `option()` — `arguments.indexOf(name)`; then `option(arguments, \"--source\")` returns the next argument. Note `--store` handling. Fine.\n\n4. In `run()`, `--password` refusal. Good.\n\n5. `localSource()` returns constant \"console-local\" — so all console logins share one source bucket. That's fine/documented.\n\n6. Big one: in `login()`, `readLine` may return `null` (console.readLine returns null on EOF). Then `username` = null → authenticate(null,...) → InputPolicy throws → denied. Fine.\n\n7. `readPassword` returns null when console.readPassword returns null (EOF) → error message \"No terminal available\". fine.\n\n8. `login`: `statePath` default = `storePath + \".attempts\"` — in same dir as credential file. OK.\n\nNow, the most important area: does the rate limiting actually work? Let me look for a subtle bug. Let's re-check `AttemptCounters.consume` and FileAttemptLimiter's transaction ordering.\n\nIn `FileAttemptLimiter.tryReserve`: consume source, then if not permitted commit & return. Else consume account, then `transaction.commit()` — always commits regardless. Good.\n\nWait — potential bug in `AttemptCounters.consume`: When state == null and map.size() >= maxKeys, prune, then check again. Fine.\n\nNow, `FileAttemptLimiter.Transaction.read()` — `ByteBuffer.allocate((int) open.size())`. Size checked <= 16MB. OK. But note: the size check happens after acquiring the lock, good.\n\nHmm, but there's a subtle TOCTOU: `PrivateFiles.requireOwnerOnly(statePath, ...)` then `FileChannel.open(...)` — attacker needs write on parent dir; requireOwnerOnly rejects world-writable parent. Group-writable parent not checked though. Minor.\n\nNow — the critical review area: `Authenticator`. Let's re-check the \"reserve before verify\" and the failure/success accounting. On success: `limiter.recordSuccess(reservation)` clears the account counter. On `sessions.create` throwing, catch → recordFailure. OK.\n\nNow a REAL issue: In `Authenticator.authenticate`, the account counter is cleared on success — standard. Source giveBack. Fine.\n\nLet's check `AttemptCounters.consume` restriction logic for an off-by-one that weakens the limit:\n\n- maxAttempts = 5. state.attempts starts 0. First call: attempts 0 >= 5? no → attempts=1, PERMITTED. So 5 attempts permitted within window, 6th → restricted. Matches \"5 attempts / 15 min\". It exists\n * because the portal's web front end wraps exactly this authenticator, and the attributes a session\n * cookie needs are a property of the session, not of the transport -- leaving them to be\n * rediscovered by whoever writes the servlet is how a session cookie ends up without\n * {@code HttpOnly}.\n *\n * <p>Every issued cookie carries:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>{@code HttpOnly} -- page script cannot read the token, so one XSS flaw elsewhere in the\n *       portal does not become session theft.</li>\n *   <li>{@code Secure} -- the token is never sent over plaintext HTTP.</li>\n *   <li>{@code SameSite=Strict} -- the cookie is not attached to cross-site requests, which removes\n *       the ambient authority that cross-site request forgery depends on.</li>\n *   <li>{@code Path=/portal} -- scoped to the application rather than the whole origin, so it is not\n *       offered to unrelated apps sharing the host.</li>\n *   <li>{@code Max-Age} matching the server-side idle timeout, so the browser and the server agree\n *       on when the session is over. The server-side record remains authoritative.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>No {@code Domain} attribute is set: omitting it keeps the cookie host-only, where setting it\n * would widen the cookie to subdomains.\n */\npublic final class SessionCookie {\n\n    /** The cookie name. A {@code __Host-} prefix additionally pins the cookie to this exact host. */\n    public static final String NAME = \"__Host-portal_session\";\n    private static final String PATH = \"/\";\n\n    private SessionCookie() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Returns the {@code Set-Cookie} value carrying {@code token}.\n     *\n     * <p>The {@code __Host-} prefix that browsers enforce requires {@code Secure}, no {@code Domain},\n     * and {@code Path=/}; those are fixed here rather than parameterised so the prefix cannot be\n     * invalidated by a caller.\n     *\n     * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the token is not one this application issued -- a shape\n     *                                 check, so a caller cannot smuggle cookie attributes through\n     *                                 the value\n     */\n    public static String setCookieHeader(String token, Duration maxAge) {\n        if (!SessionManager.hasTokenShape(token)) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"refusing to build a cookie for a malformed token\");\n        }\n        if (maxAge == null || maxAge.isNegative() || maxAge.isZero()) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie Max-Age must be a positive duration\");\n        }\n        return NAME + \"=\" + token\n                + \"; Path=\" + PATH\n                + \"; Max-Age=\" + maxAge.toSeconds()\n                + \"; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict\";\n    }\n\n    /** Returns the header value that clears the cookie, for use on sign-out. */\n    public static String clearCookieHeader() {\n        return NAME + \"=; Honestly, a realistic recommendation: erase the raw token bytes after encoding, and don't retain the token in long-lived objects (AuthResult could hold only the digest-keyed session).
